How to read this report
  • A run's score is the weighted fraction of its graders that passed; a "perfect" run passed every grader.
  • A case's score is the mean of its runs; a case passes when its score is at or above the 100% threshold (the tick on each case bar).
  • The suite score is the mean of the per-case scores.
  • Judge votes are independent samples of the LLM judge; the majority decides pass or fail.
  • "With plugin" and "Baseline" runs are identical except for the plugin being loaded; Δ is the with-plugin score minus the baseline score.

trg-07-l1-no-corner

evals/tune-regularization/trg-07-l1-no-corner · 0.0 pts with plugin 93%

Run 1 100% 9 turns · $1.65 · 20:21:22 UTC
✓ pass criterion

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I recommend **`eps = 0.63`**. I did not get it from the L-curve, because your L-curve has no corner.

## What I ran
`sweep.py` runs `fista` at 40 log-spaced values of `eps`, from just below `eps_max = 2·max|Opᴴy| = 4.18` (above that, x = 0) down to 4e-4. Each solve uses 3000 iterations and a fixed step size, and starts from the previous solution. Restarting from zero at two of those values gave the same answers, so the solves have converged. The script saves `lcurve_fista.png`, which shows the L-curve, the residual and SNR against `eps`, and the models.

## Why there is no L-curve corner
Plotted on log axes, the L1 norm of the model against the data residual bends the same way everywhere (curvature ≤ 0 over 4 decades). There is no switch from a steep part to a flat part, so there is no corner. The highest curvature falls at the end of the grid (`eps ≈ 5e-4`, 80 non-zeros, fitting the noise exactly), which is meaningless. This often happens when the penalty is L1. The script now detects this case and reports it instead of returning a value.

## Why 0.63
You know the noise level, so I used the discrepancy principle instead. It picks the largest `eps` whose residual equals the noise level: ‖y − Op x‖ = σ√M = 0.15·√80 = 1.342. I refined it with bisection to `eps = 0.631`:

| eps | ‖r‖ | non-zeros | SNR vs xtrue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.0 (yours) | ~2.44 | 4–5 | 3.8 dB |
| **0.63 (discrepancy)** | 1.342 | 10 | 10.7 dB |
| 0.29 (best SNR, check only) | 0.93 | 36 | 12.3 dB |

- **`eps = 2.0` is too strong.** Its residual is about 1.8 times the noise level, so it misses real spikes and shrinks the ones it keeps.
- **`eps = 0.63` finds all 6 true spikes**, plus a few small false ones.
- **The best-SNR value (0.29)** fits the noise a little (residual below σ√M) and gives 36 non-zeros. You can only compute it because this is a synthetic test, so I didn't use it for the pick.

The change to make in your script:
```python
xinv = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(Op, y, eps=0.63, niter=500)[0]
```

Two caveats:
- With 500 iterations and no `alpha` given, the result will be close to the sweep's but may not match it exactly.
- L1 shrinks the amplitudes. If you want sparse output with correct amplitudes, a least-squares refit on the support found at `eps = 0.63` would remove that bias.

The script's last printed line is `EPS_CHOSEN=0.63`.

The script's last printed line is `EPS_CHOSEN=0.63`.

Check the environment\n\nSame as the other PyLops skills: use the project's Python runner, check\n`pylops.__version__`, inspect solver signatures in the installed version.\n\n## 1. Identify the parameter(s)\n\nMap the user's solver to the knob being tuned and its meaning:\n\n| Solver | Parameter | Objective term |\n|---|---|---|\n| `cgls` / `lsqr` | `damp` | `damp² ‖x‖²` (for `lsqr` only with `x0=None`, see step 3) |\n| `regularized_inversion` | `epsRs=[ε₁, ε₂, …]` | `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` |\n| `normal_equations_inversion` | `epsI`, `epsRs` (and `epsNRs`) | `epsI² ‖x‖²`, `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` (same scaling as `regularized_inversion`) |\n| `ista` / `fista` | `eps` | `eps ‖SOpᴴ x‖₁` (effective threshold is `eps·alpha/2`) |\n| `splitbregman` | `epsRL1s`, `epsRL2s`, `mu` | L1 and L2 weights, data weight |\n| `spgl1` | `sigma` (BPDN) or `tau` (LASSO) | residual bound or L1-ball radius |\n| any Krylov solver without explicit regularisation | `niter` | early stopping (semi-convergence) |\n\nWith more than one weight, tune one at a time (fix the others at a\nscale-based guess), or fix their ratio and sweep an overall factor. Do not\nlaunch a full grid over several parameters unless the problem is small.\n\n## 2. Pick the criterion\n\nChoose based on what the user knows:\n\n1. **Known noise level** (sigma per sample, or an estimate from a noise-only\n   window): **discrepancy principle** - choose the largest regularisation whose\n   data residual `‖y − Op x‖` is ≤ `τ · σ · sqrt(N)`, `τ ≈ 1.0–1.1`. For\n   `spgl1`, this is exactly `sigma = τ σ sqrt(N)`.\n2. **Synthetic with a true model**: maximise `pylops.utils.metrics.snr(xtrue, x)`\n   (report it, but warn it does not transfer 1:1 to field data).\n3. **Nothing known**: **L-curve** - plot `log ‖Rx‖` vs `log ‖y − Op x‖` and pick the\n   point of maximum curvature. Needs a wide enough range to show both branches.\n4. **Small problem** (`Op.todense()` affordable, N, M ≲ 3000) and quadratic\n   regularisation: **GCV** computed exactly on the dense matrices (implemented in\n   the template).\n\nReport more than one criterion when they are cheap; disagreement between them is\ninformation for the user.\n\n## 3. Write and run the sweep\n\nAdapt `reference/lcurve_template.py`:\n\n- replace `build_problem` with the user's operator, data and regularisation;\n- replace `solve(eps, x0)` with the user's solver call **with everything else\n  fixed** (same `niter`, tolerance, `x0` policy);\n- use a **log-spaced grid** spanning at least 4 orders of magnitude around a\n  scale-based guess (e.g. `ε₀ ≈ ‖Opᴴ y‖ / ‖Rᴴ R Opᴴ y‖` for quadratic terms, or\n  `eps₀ ≈ max|Opᴴ y|` scale for L1 thresholds, where larger values give all-zero\n  models);\n- solve from large to small ε and **warm start** from the previous solution\n  (`x0=...`) to save iterations - unless the solver ignores `x0`. **Exception:\n  `damp` of `lsqr`** (PyLops and SciPy): with `x0` it solves for the correction\n  `x - x0`, so it penalises `damp² ‖x - x0‖²`, not `damp² ‖x‖²`, and a\n  warm-started sweep is biased (residual too low, discrepancy pick too large).\n  Use `x0=None` for every `lsqr(damp=...)` solve, or put the penalty in the\n  operator (`VStack([Op, eps*Reg])`, `regularized_inversion`), where `x0` is\n  safe. `cgls(damp=...)` and `epsRs` are not affected (checked numerically);\n- keep `niter` large enough that each solve has converged, otherwise the curve\n  measures iteration count, not regularisation (check a couple of cost curves);\n- for early stopping as regulariser, run once with a callback that stores\n  `x` every k iterations and compute the same criteria on iteration number.\n\nRun it, then look at the plot before trusting the automatic pick: an L-curve with\nno clear corner (common with L1 terms or very noisy data) should be reported as\nsuch, not forced. With derivative regularisers a second knee often appears at\nvery large ε (the model collapses into the null space of `R`, e.g. a linear\ntrend for `SecondDerivative`); it bends the other way and is not the L-curve\ncorner – the template's curvature orientation already excludes it.\n\n## 4. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01H4qeZL7bQFQGncFYBhvuTs","type":"tool_result","content":"\"\"\"Template for choosing a regularisation weight in a PyLops inversion.\n\nRuns as-is on a toy 1-D deconvolution with second-derivative (smoothness)\nregularisation. To adapt it, replace ``build_problem`` and ``solve`` and keep\nthe sweep, the selection criteria and the plots.\n\nCriteria implemented:\n- L-curve corner (maximum curvature in log-log);\n- discrepancy principle (needs the noise standard deviation);\n- best SNR against a known model (synthetic tests only);\n- GCV, exact on dense matrices (small quadratic problems only).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport numpy as np\n\nimport pylops\nfrom pylops.utils.metrics import snr\n\n\ndef build_problem(rng):\n    \"\"\"ADAPT: return (Op, Reg, y, xtrue or None, noise_std or None).\"\"\"\n    nt = 201\n    t = np.arange(nt) * 0.004\n    h, _, hcenter = pylops.utils.wavelets.ricker(t[:41], f0=15)\n    Op = pylops.signalprocessing.Convolve1D(nt, h=h, offset=hcenter)\n    Reg = pylops.SecondDerivative(nt)\n\n    xtrue = np.sin(2 * np.pi * 3 * t) * np.exp(-t)  # smooth model\n    noise_std = 0.05\n    y = Op @ xtrue + noise_std * rng.standard_normal(nt)\n    return Op, Reg, y, xtrue, noise_std\n\n\ndef solve(Op, Reg, y, eps, x0):\n    \"\"\"ADAPT: the user's solver call with everything but ``eps`` fixed.\"\"\"\n    Opaug = pylops.VStack([Op, eps * Reg])\n    yaug = np.concatenate([y, np.zeros(Reg.shape[0])])\n    return pylops.cgls(Opaug, yaug, x0=x0, niter=200, tol=1e-10)[0]\n\n\ndef lcurve_corner(epsilons, rnorm, xnorm):\n    \"\"\"Index of the L-curve corner (maximum curvature in log-log).\n\n    Points are ordered by increasing eps so that the corner between the\n    \"under-regularised\" (vertical) and \"over-regularised\" (horizontal) branches\n    has positive curvature. A second knee with opposite curvature can appear\n    at very large eps (model pushed into the null space of the regulariser):\n    it is not the L-curve corner.\n    \"\"\"\n    order = np.argsort(epsilons)\n    lr, lx = np.log(rnorm[order]), np.log(xnorm[order])\n    # parametrise by arc length to make finite differences uniform\n    s = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(np.hypot(np.diff(lr), np.diff(lx)))])\n    dr, dx = np.gradient(lr, s), np.gradient(lx, s)\n    ddr, ddx = np.gradient(dr, s), np.gradient(dx, s)\n    kappa = (dr * ddx - ddr * dx) / (dr**2 + dx**2) ** 1.5\n    kappa[[0, -1]] = -np.inf  # ends are not valid corners\n    kappa_orig = np.empty_like(kappa)\n    kappa_orig[order] = kappa\n    return int(np.argmax(kappa_orig)), kappa_orig\n\n\ndef discrepancy(epsilons, rnorm, noise_std, n, tau=1.02):\n    \"\"\"Largest eps whose residual is below tau * nois

"""Choose eps for pylops fista (L1 sparsity) on the user's test problem.

fista minimises ||y - Op x||_2^2 + eps ||x||_1 (soft threshold eps*alpha/2),
so eps >= eps_max = 2 max|Op^H y| gives x = 0. We sweep eps on a log grid
below eps_max (large -> small, warm-started), and evaluate:
  - L-curve corner: max curvature of log||x||_1 vs log||y - Op x||_2
  - discrepancy principle: largest eps with ||r|| <= tau * sigma * sqrt(M)
  - best SNR against xtrue (synthetic-only check)
"""

import matplotlib

matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

import pylops
from pylops.utils.metrics import snr

# ---------------- user's problem (unchanged) ----------------
rng = np.random.default_rng(2)
N, M = 200, 80
A = rng.standard_normal((M, N)) / np.sqrt(M)
Op = pylops.MatrixMult(A)
xtrue = np.zeros(N)
xtrue[rng.choice(N, 6, replace=False)] = rng.uniform(1, 3, 6) * rng.choice([-1, 1], 6)
sigma = 0.15
y = Op @ xtrue + sigma * rng.standard_normal(M)

# fixed solver settings (only eps changes); fixed step for reproducibility
alpha = 1.0 / np.linalg.norm(A, 2) ** 2
NITER = 3000


def solve(eps, x0):
    return pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(
        Op, y, x0=x0, eps=eps, alpha=alpha, niter=NITER, tol=1e-12
    )[0]


def lcurve_corner(epsilons, rnorm, xnorm):
    """Max-curvature point of the log-log L-curve (ordered by increasing eps)."""
    order = np.argsort(epsilons)
    lr, lx = np.log(rnorm[order]), np.log(xnorm[order])
    s = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(np.hypot(np.diff(lr), np.diff(lx)))])
    dr, dx = np.gradient(lr, s), np.gradient(lx, s)
    ddr, ddx = np.gradient(dr, s), np.gradient(dx, s)
    kappa = (dr * ddx - ddr * dx) / (dr**2 + dx**2) ** 1.5
    kappa[[0, -1]] = -np.inf
    out = np.empty_like(kappa)
    out[order] = kappa
    return int(np.argmax(out)), out


# ---------------- sweep ----------------
eps_max = 2 * np.max(np.abs(Op.H @ y))
neps = 40
epsilons = eps_max * np.logspace(-0.05, -4, neps)  # just below eps_max down to 1e-4*eps_max

xs, rnorm, xnorm, nnz = [], [], [], []
x0 = np.zeros(N)
for eps in epsilons:
    x = solve(eps, x0)
    x0 = x
    xs.append(x)
    rnorm.append(np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ x))
    xnorm.append(np.linalg.norm(x, 1))
    nnz.append(int(np.sum(np.abs(x) > 1e-6)))
rnorm, xnorm, nnz = map(np.array, (rnorm, xnorm, nnz))
snrs = np.array([snr(xtrue, x) for x in xs])

# convergence sanity check: re-solve two points from scratch, compare
for i in (neps // 2, neps - 1):
    xc = solve(epsilons[i], None)
    print(f"cold vs warm start at eps={epsilons[i]:.3g}: "
          f"rel diff {np.linalg.norm(xc - xs[i]) / np.linalg.norm(xs[i]):.1e}")

icorner, kappa = lcurve_corner(epsilons, rnorm, xnorm)
target = 1.0 * sigma * np.sqrt(M)
ok = np.where(rnorm <= target)[0]
idisc = int(ok[np.argmax(epsilons[ok])]) if ok.size else None
isnr = int(np.argmax(snrs))
i2 = int(np.argmin(np.abs(epsilons - 2.0)))

print(f"\neps_max (x=0 above) = {eps_max:.3f}, noise level sigma*sqrt(M) = {target:.3f}")
print(f"{'eps':>9} {'||r||':>8} {'||x||1':>8} {'nnz':>4} {'SNR':>6} {'kappa':>7}")
for i, e in enumerate(epsilons):
    tag = []
    if i == icorner: tag.append("<- max curvature (grid edge, see below)")
    if i == idisc: tag.append("<- discrepancy")
    if i == isnr: tag.append("<- best SNR")
    print(f"{e:9.4f} {rnorm[i]:8.3f} {xnorm[i]:8.3f} {nnz[i]:4d} {snrs[i]:6.2f} "
          f"{kappa[i]:7.2f} {' '.join(tag)}")

# An L-curve corner needs positive curvature (vertical -> horizontal branch).
has_corner = kappa[icorner] > 0.05
if not has_corner:
    print(f"\nL-curve: NO corner - max curvature {kappa[icorner]:.3f} <= 0 over the whole "
          "grid (curve is concave in log-log, typical for ||x||_1 vs ||r||); "
          "the argmax sits at the grid edge and is not a valid pick.")
picks = {"best SNR": isnr}
if has_corner:
    picks["L-curve"] = icorner
if idisc is not None:
    picks["discrepancy"] = idisc
print()
for k, i in picks.items():
    print(f"{k:12s}: eps={epsilons[i]:.4f}, ||r||={rnorm[i]:.3f}, nnz={nnz[i]}, SNR={snrs[i]:.2f} dB")
print(f"{'eps=2.0 (yours)':12s}: ||r||~{rnorm[i2]:.3f}, nnz={nnz[i2]}, SNR={snrs[i2]:.2f} dB (nearest grid eps={epsilons[i2]:.3f})")

# ---------------- plots ----------------
fig, axs = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(16, 4.5))
axs[0].loglog(rnorm, xnorm, ".-k")
for k, i in picks.items():
    axs[0].loglog(rnorm[i], xnorm[i], "o", ms=9, label=f"{k} eps={epsilons[i]:.3g}")
axs[0].axvline(target, color="r", ls="--", lw=1, label="sigma*sqrt(M)")
axs[0].set_xlabel("||y - Op x||_2"); axs[0].set_ylabel("||x||_1"); axs[0].set_title("L-curve")
axs[0].legend(fontsize=8)
axs[1].loglog(epsilons, rnorm, ".-k", label="||r||")
axs[1].axhline(target, color="r", ls="--", label="sigma*sqrt(M)")
axs[1].set_xlabel("eps"); axs[1].legend(loc="upper left")
ax2 = axs[1].twinx(); ax2.semilogx(epsilons, snrs, ".-b"); ax2.set_ylabel("SNR [dB]", color="b")
axs[2].stem(xtrue, linefmt="k-", markerfmt="ko", basefmt=" ", label="true")
chosen = picks.get("discrepancy", isnr)
for name, i in [("under-reg", min(neps - 1, chosen + 8)), ("chosen", chosen), ("over-reg", max(0, chosen - 8))]:
    axs[2].plot(xs[i], ".", label=f"{name} eps={epsilons[i]:.2g}")
axs[2].legend(fontsize=8)
fig.tight_layout()
fig.savefig("lcurve_fista.png", dpi=120)
print("saved lcurve_fista.png")

# ---------------- refine discrepancy pick by bisection in log(eps) ----------------
# ||r(eps)|| is monotone in eps: find eps with ||r|| = sigma*sqrt(M)
if idisc is None or idisc == 0:
    raise SystemExit("discrepancy target not bracketed; extend the grid")
lo, hi = np.log(epsilons[idisc]), np.log(epsilons[idisc - 1])  # r(lo) <= target < r(hi)
xw = xs[idisc]
for _ in range(12):
    mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
    xm = solve(np.exp(mid), xw)
    if np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ xm) <= target:
        lo, xw = mid, xm
    else:
        hi = mid
eps_chosen = float(np.exp(lo))
xch = solve(eps_chosen, None)
print(f"\nrefined discrepancy: eps={eps_chosen:.3f}, ||r||={np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ xch):.3f} "
      f"(target {target:.3f}), nnz={int(np.sum(np.abs(xch) > 1e-6))}, SNR={snr(xtrue, xch):.2f} dB")
print(f"EPS_CHOSEN={eps_chosen:.2f}")

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /Users/mrava87/Desktop/OpenSource/pylops-agent-skills/plugins/pylops/skills/tune-regularization\n\nGoal: a **sweep script** in the user's project that evaluates a regularisation\nparameter over a range, applies one or more selection criteria, and reports the\nchosen value with a plot that justifies it. PyLops has no built-in L-curve / GCV /\ndiscrepancy helper, so the skill provides `reference/lcurve_template.py`.\n\nRequires a working inversion (operator passes the dot-test, solver runs). If there\nis none, use `solve-inverse-problem` first.\n\n## 0. Check the environment\n\nSame as the other PyLops skills: use the project's Python runner, check\n`pylops.__version__`, inspect solver signatures in the installed version.\n\n## 1. Identify the parameter(s)\n\nMap the user's solver to the knob being tuned and its meaning:\n\n| Solver | Parameter | Objective term |\n|---|---|---|\n| `cgls` / `lsqr` | `damp` | `damp² ‖x‖²` (for `lsqr` only with `x0=None`, see step 3) |\n| `regularized_inversion` | `epsRs=[ε₁, ε₂, …]` | `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` |\n| `normal_equations_inversion` | `epsI`, `epsRs` (and `epsNRs`) | `epsI² ‖x‖²`, `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` (same scaling as `regularized_inversion`) |\n| `ista` / `fista` | `eps` | `eps ‖SOpᴴ x‖₁` (effective threshold is `eps·alpha/2`) |\n| `splitbregman` | `epsRL1s`, `epsRL2s`, `mu` | L1 and L2 weights, data weight |\n| `spgl1` | `sigma` (BPDN) or `tau` (LASSO) | residual bound or L1-ball radius |\n| any Krylov solver without explicit regularisation | `niter` | early stopping (semi-convergence) |\n\nWith more than one weight, tune one at a time (fix the others at a\nscale-based guess), or fix their ratio and sweep an overall factor. Do not\nlaunch a full grid over several parameters unless the problem is small.\n\n## 2. Pick the criterion\n\nChoose based on what the user knows:\n\n1. **Known noise level** (sigma per sample, or an estimate from a noise-only\n   window): **discrepancy principle** - choose the largest regularisation whose\n   data residual `‖y − Op x‖` is ≤ `τ · σ · sqrt(N)`, `τ ≈ 1.0–1.1`. For\n   `spgl1`, this is exactly `sigma = τ σ sqrt(N)`.\n2. **Synthetic with a true model**: maximise `pylops.utils.metrics.snr(xtrue, x)`\n   (report it, but warn it does not transfer 1:1 to field data).\n3. **Nothing known**: **L-curve** - plot `log ‖Rx‖` vs `log ‖y − Op x‖` and pick the\n   point of maximum curvature. Needs a wide enough range to show both branches.\n4. **Small problem** (`Op.todense()` affordable, N, M ≲ 3000) and quadratic\n   regularisation: **GCV** computed exactly on the dense matrices (implemented in\n   the template).\n\nReport more than one criterion when they are cheap; disagreement between them is\ninformation for the user.\n\n## 3. Write and run the sweep\n\nAdapt `reference/lcurve_template.py`:\n\n- replace `build_problem` with the user's operator, data and regularisation;\n- replace `solve(eps, x0)` with the user's solver call **with everything else\n  fixed** (same `niter`, tolerance, `x0` policy);\n- use a **log-spaced grid** spanning at least 4 orders of magnitude around a\n  scale-based guess (e.g. `ε₀ ≈ ‖Opᴴ y‖ / ‖Rᴴ R Opᴴ y‖` for quadratic terms, or\n  `eps₀ ≈ max|Opᴴ y|` scale for L1 thresholds, where larger values give all-zero\n  models);\n- solve from large to small ε and **warm start** from the previous solution\n  (`x0=...`) to save iterations - unless the solver ignores `x0`. **Exception:\n  `damp` of `lsqr`** (PyLops and SciPy): with `x0` it solves for the correction\n  `x - x0`, so it penalises `damp² ‖x - x0‖²`, not `damp² ‖x‖²`, and a\n  warm-started sweep is biased (residual too low, discrepancy pick too large).\n  Use `x0=None` for every `lsqr(damp=...)` solve, or put the penalty in the\n  operator (`VStack([Op, eps*Reg])`, `regularized_inversion`), where `x0` is\n  safe. `cgls(damp=...)` and `epsRs` are not affected (checked numerically);\n- keep `niter` large enough that each solve has converged, otherwise the curve\n  measures iteration count, not regularisation (check a couple of cost curves);\n- for early stopping as regulariser, run once with a callback that stores\n  `x` every k iterations and compute the same criteria on iteration number.\n\nRun it, then look at the plot before trusting the automatic pick: an L-curve with\nno clear corner (common with L1 terms or very noisy data) should be reported as\nsuch, not forced. With derivative regularisers a second knee often appears at\nvery large ε (the model collapses into the null space of `R`, e.g. a linear\ntrend for `SecondDerivative`); it bends the other way and is not the L-curve\ncorner – the template's curvature orientation already excludes it.\n\n## 4. Hand off\n\nReport:\n\n- the parameter swept, the range and the number of solves;\n- the value picked by each criterion and the one recommended, with the reason;\n- the plot file(s) (L-curve, residual vs ε, SNR vs ε if available) and the\n  model at the chosen value next to one over- and one under-regularised model;\n- the exact line to change in the user's inversion script.\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b9c1dac2-e40f-4023-9fd8-414d542a6452","uuid":"05c58b67-08a0-4432-9ed8-b18e6369a697","timestamp":"2026-11-01T20:21:25.168Z","isSynthetic":true}

A second knee with opposite curvature can appear\n    at very large eps (model pushed into the null space of the regulariser):\n    it is not the L-curve corner.\n    \"\"\"\n    order = np.argsort(epsilons)\n    lr, lx = np.log(rnorm[order]), np.log(xnorm[order])\n    # parametrise by arc length to make finite differences uniform\n    s = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(np.hypot(np.diff(lr), np.diff(lx)))])\n    dr, dx = np.gradient(lr, s), np.gradient(lx, s)\n    ddr, ddx = np.gradient(dr, s), np.gradient(dx, s)\n    kappa = (dr * ddx - ddr * dx) / (dr**2 + dx**2) ** 1.5\n    kappa[[0, -1]] = -np.inf  # ends are not valid corners\n    kappa_orig = np.empty_like(kappa)\n    kappa_orig[order] = kappa\n    return int(np.argmax(kappa_orig)), kappa_orig\n\n\ndef discrepancy(epsilons, rnorm, noise_std, n, tau=1.02):\n    \"\"\"Largest eps whose residual is below tau * nois

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I can't give you a corner, because this L-curve doesn't have one. I chose **`eps ≈ 0.63`** using the discrepancy principle instead, which works here because you know the noise level.

**What I ran.** `sweep.py` runs `fista` at 60 values of `eps`, spaced evenly on a log scale from 4.18 down to 4e-4. Above about 4.18 the model is all zeros. Each run uses 5000 iterations and starts from the previous run's solution. I re-ran three of them from scratch to check they had converged; the results matched (differences of 1e-10 to 1e-4). The script ran without errors and saved the plots to `lcurve_sweep.png`.

**The L-curve has no corner.** Plotted as `‖y − Op x‖₂` against `‖x‖₁` on log axes, the points form a smooth curve that bends the wrong way, with no "L" shape. A corner needs positive curvature somewhere, and the curvature is zero or negative at every point (largest value −0.001). A "maximum curvature" rule would just pick the smallest `eps` I tried (0.0005), which fits the noise: 80 non-zeros and 7.4 dB SNR. This often happens with an L1 penalty, because `‖x‖₁` changes almost linearly with `eps` near the useful range. The script detects this and prints "NO CORNER" rather than reporting a fake one.

**Why the discrepancy principle instead.** With σ = 0.15 and M = 80, the expected size of the noise residual is σ·√M ≈ 1.34. The rule picks the largest `eps` whose residual is no bigger than that:

| method | eps | ‖r‖ | non-zeros | SNR vs xtrue |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **discrepancy (chosen)** | **0.63** | 1.34 | 10 | 10.7 dB |
| best SNR (only possible because you know xtrue) | 0.25 | 0.85 | 44 | 12.3 dB |
| your original | 2.0 | ≈2.6 | 4 | ≈2.5 dB |
| smallest eps tried | 5e-4 | 0.002 | 80 | 7.4 dB |

At `eps = 0.63` all 6 true spikes are found, plus 4 tiny extra values. The spike sizes come out shrunk by about 20–30%, which is normal for L1. Your `eps = 2.0` misses two spikes and leaves a residual about twice the noise level.

The best-SNR value (0.25) needs the true model, so you couldn't use it on real data. It also gives 44 non-zeros, which is a poor sparse answer.

**Change to make in your code:**
```python
xinv = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(Op, y, eps=0.63, niter=500)[0]
```
At this `eps`, 500 iterations may not be enough to fully converge, so use `niter` of 2000 or more. If the amplitudes matter, a common follow-up is to keep the 6–10 non-zero positions and re-fit their values by least squares, which removes most of the shrinkage.
✓ pass final-value ×0.5

Do not\nlaunch a full grid over several parameters unless the problem is small.\n\n## 2. For\n   `spgl1`, this is exactly `sigma = τ σ sqrt(N)`.\n2. **Synthetic with a true model**: maximise `pylops.utils.metrics.snr(xtrue, x)`\n   (report it, but warn it does not transfer 1:1 to field data).\n3. **Nothing known**: **L-curve** - plot `log ‖Rx‖` vs `log ‖y − Op x‖` and pick the\n   point of maximum curvature. To adapt it, replace ``build_problem`` and ``solve`` and keep\nthe sweep, the selection criteria and the plots.\n\nCriteria implemented:\n- L-curve corner (maximum curvature in log-log);\n- discrepancy principle (needs the noise standard deviation);\n- best SNR against a known model (synthetic tests only);\n- GCV, exact on dense matrices (small quadratic problems only).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport numpy as np\n\nimport pylops\nfrom pylops.utils.metrics import snr\n\n\ndef build_problem(rng):\n    \"\"\"ADAPT: return (Op, Reg, y, xtrue or None, noise_std or None).\"\"\"\n    nt = 201\n    t = np.arange(nt) * 0.004\n    h, _, hcenter = pylops.utils.wavelets.ricker(t[:41], f0=15)\n    Op = pylops.signalprocessing.Convolve1D(nt, h=h, offset=hcenter)\n    Reg = pylops.SecondDerivative(nt)\n\n    xtrue = np.sin(2 * np.pi * 3 * t) * np.exp(-t)  # smooth model\n    noise_std = 0.05\n    y = Op @ xtrue + noise_std * rng.standard_normal(nt)\n    return Op, Reg, y, xtrue, noise_std\n\n\ndef solve(Op, Reg, y, eps, x0):\n    \"\"\"ADAPT: the user's solver call with everything but ``eps`` fixed.\"\"\"\n    Opaug = pylops.VStack([Op, eps * Reg])\n    yaug = np.concatenate([y, np.zeros(Reg.shape[0])])\n    return pylops.cgls(Opaug, yaug, x0=x0, niter=200, tol=1e-10)[0]\n\n\ndef lcurve_corner(epsilons, rnorm, xnorm):\n    \"\"\"Index of the L-curve corner (maximum curvature in log-log).\n\n    Points are ordered by increasing eps so that the corner between the\n    \"under-regularised\" (vertical) and \"over-regularised\" (horizontal) branches\n    has positive curvature. """Choose eps for pylops fista on a sparse-recovery test problem.

Sweeps eps on a log grid, builds the L-curve (||y - Op x||_2 vs ||x||_1),
locates its maximum-curvature point, and compares it with the discrepancy
principle (noise std known) and with the best SNR against xtrue.
"""

import matplotlib

matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

import pylops
from pylops.utils.metrics import snr

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- problem
rng = np.random.default_rng(2)
N, M = 200, 80
A = rng.standard_normal((M, N)) / np.sqrt(M)
Op = pylops.MatrixMult(A)
xtrue = np.zeros(N)
xtrue[rng.choice(N, 6, replace=False)] = rng.uniform(1, 3, 6) * rng.choice([-1, 1], 6)
sigma = 0.15
y = Op @ xtrue + sigma * rng.standard_normal(M)

NITER = 5000  # large enough that every solve on the grid has converged
TOL = 1e-12


def solve(eps, x0):
    return pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(
        Op, y, x0=x0, eps=eps, niter=NITER, tol=TOL
    )[0]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- sweep
# PyLops fista minimises ||y - Ax||^2 + eps ||x||_1 (threshold eps*alpha/2),
# so x = 0 for eps >= eps_max = 2 max|A^T y|.
eps_max = 2 * np.max(np.abs(Op.H @ y))
epsilons = eps_max * np.logspace(-0.01, -4, 60)  # large -> small, warm start

xs, rnorm, l1norm, nnz = [], [], [], []
x0 = np.zeros(N)
for eps in epsilons:
    x = solve(eps, x0)
    x0 = x
    xs.append(x)
    rnorm.append(np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ x))
    l1norm.append(np.sum(np.abs(x)))
    nnz.append(int(np.sum(np.abs(x) > 1e-6)))
rnorm, l1norm, nnz = map(np.array, (rnorm, l1norm, nnz))
snrs = np.array([snr(xtrue, x) for x in xs])

# convergence check: re-solve a few points cold with 2x iterations
for i in (10, 30, 50):
    xc = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(
        Op, y, eps=epsilons[i], niter=2 * NITER, tol=TOL
    )[0]
    print(f"conv check eps={epsilons[i]:.3e}: "
          f"rel diff warm/cold = {np.linalg.norm(xc - xs[i]) / max(np.linalg.norm(xc), 1e-12):.1e}")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- criteria
def lcurve_curvature(eps, r, m):
    """Signed curvature of (log r, log m) ordered by increasing eps."""
    order = np.argsort(eps)
    lr, lm = np.log(r[order]), np.log(m[order])
    s = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(np.hypot(np.diff(lr), np.diff(lm)))])
    dr, dm = np.gradient(lr, s), np.gradient(lm, s)
    ddr, ddm = np.gradient(dr, s), np.gradient(dm, s)
    k = (dr * ddm - ddr * dm) / (dr**2 + dm**2) ** 1.5
    k[[0, -1]] = -np.inf
    out = np.empty_like(k)
    out[order] = k
    return out


valid = l1norm > 0  # log of ||x||_1 undefined at x=0
kappa = np.full(epsilons.size, -np.inf)
kappa[valid] = lcurve_curvature(epsilons[valid], rnorm[valid], l1norm[valid])
# An L-curve corner needs positive curvature (convex "L" in log-log).
has_corner = np.nanmax(kappa) > 0
icorner = int(np.argmax(kappa)) if has_corner else None

target = 1.0 * sigma * np.sqrt(M)
ok = np.where(rnorm <= target)[0]
idisc = int(ok[np.argmax(epsilons[ok])])
isnr = int(np.argmax(snrs))

print(f"\neps_max (x=0 above) = {eps_max:.3f}; grid {epsilons.min():.2e}..{epsilons.max():.2e}, "
      f"{epsilons.size} solves")
print(f"noise level sigma*sqrt(M) = {target:.3f}")
print(f"{'eps':>10} {'||r||':>8} {'||x||1':>8} {'nnz':>4} {'SNR':>6} {'kappa':>7}")
for i in range(0, epsilons.size, 3):
    print(f"{epsilons[i]:10.4f} {rnorm[i]:8.3f} {l1norm[i]:8.2f} {nnz[i]:4d} "
          f"{snrs[i]:6.2f} {kappa[i]:7.2f}")
print()
picks = [("discrepancy", idisc), ("best SNR", isnr)]
if has_corner:
    picks.insert(0, ("L-curve", icorner))
else:
    print(f"L-curve: NO CORNER - curvature <= 0 everywhere (max kappa = "
          f"{np.nanmax(kappa[np.isfinite(kappa)]):.3f}); the L1 L-curve is a concave arc, "
          "so it cannot select eps here.")
for name, i in picks:
    print(f"{name:12s}: eps={epsilons[i]:.4f}  ||r||={rnorm[i]:.3f}  ||x||1={l1norm[i]:.2f}  "
          f"nnz={nnz[i]}  SNR={snrs[i]:.2f} dB")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- plots
fig, axs = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(16, 4.5))
axs[0].loglog(rnorm[valid], l1norm[valid], ".-k")
for name, i in picks:
    c = {"L-curve": "C0", "discrepancy": "C1", "best SNR": "C2"}[name]
    axs[0].loglog(rnorm[i], l1norm[i], "o", c=c, ms=9, label=f"{name} eps={epsilons[i]:.3f}")
axs[0].axvline(target, c="r", ls="--", lw=1, label="sigma*sqrt(M)")
axs[0].set_xlabel("||y - Op x||_2"); axs[0].set_ylabel("||x||_1")
axs[0].set_title("L-curve" + ("" if has_corner else " (no corner: concave)")); axs[0].legend(fontsize=8)

axs[1].loglog(epsilons, rnorm, ".-k", label="||r||")
axs[1].axhline(target, c="r", ls="--", label="noise level")
axs[1].axvline(epsilons[idisc], c="C1", ls=":")
ax2 = axs[1].twinx()
ax2.semilogx(epsilons, snrs, ".-b"); ax2.set_ylabel("SNR [dB]", color="b")
axs[1].set_xlabel("eps"); axs[1].legend(loc="lower right")

axs[2].stem(xtrue, linefmt="k-", markerfmt="ko", basefmt=" ", label="true")
for name, i, m in [("discrepancy", idisc, "C1x"), ("eps=2.0 (orig)",
                   int(np.argmin(np.abs(epsilons - 2.0))), "C3+")]:
    axs[2].plot(xs[i], m, label=f"{name}, eps={epsilons[i]:.2f}")
axs[2].legend(fontsize=8)
fig.tight_layout()
fig.savefig("lcurve_sweep.png", dpi=120)

EPS_CHOSEN = epsilons[idisc]
print(f"EPS_CHOSEN={EPS_CHOSEN:.4f}")

PyLops has no built-in L-curve / GCV /\ndiscrepancy helper, so the skill provides `reference/lcurve_template.py`.\n\nRequires a working inversion (operator passes the dot-test, solver runs). Check the environment\n\nSame as the other PyLops skills: use the project's Python runner, check\n`pylops.__version__`, inspect solver signatures in the installed version.\n\n## 1. Identify the parameter(s)\n\nMap the user's solver to the knob being tuned and its meaning:\n\n| Solver | Parameter | Objective term |\n|---|---|---|\n| `cgls` / `lsqr` | `damp` | `damp² ‖x‖²` (for `lsqr` only with `x0=None`, see step 3) |\n| `regularized_inversion` | `epsRs=[ε₁, ε₂, …]` | `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` |\n| `normal_equations_inversion` | `epsI`, `epsRs` (and `epsNRs`) | `epsI² ‖x‖²`, `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` (same scaling as `regularized_inversion`) |\n| `ista` / `fista` | `eps` | `eps ‖SOpᴴ x‖₁` (effective threshold is `eps·alpha/2`) |\n| `splitbregman` | `epsRL1s`, `epsRL2s`, `mu` | L1 and L2 weights, data weight |\n| `spgl1` | `sigma` (BPDN) or `tau` (LASSO) | residual bound or L1-ball radius |\n| any Krylov solver without explicit regularisation | `niter` | early stopping (semi-convergence) |\n\nWith more than one weight, tune one at a time (fix the others at a\nscale-based guess), or fix their ratio and sweep an overall factor. Do not\nlaunch a full grid over several parameters unless the problem is small.\n\n## 2. Pick the criterion\n\nChoose based on what the user knows:\n\n1. **Known noise level** (sigma per sample, or an estimate from a noise-only\n   window): **discrepancy principle** - choose the largest regularisation whose\n   data residual `‖y − Op x‖` is ≤ `τ · σ · sqrt(N)`, `τ ≈ 1.0–1.1`. For\n   `spgl1`, this is exactly `sigma = τ σ sqrt(N)`.\n2. **Synthetic with a true model**: maximise `pylops.utils.metrics.snr(xtrue, x)`\n   (report it, but warn it does not transfer 1:1 to field data).\n3. **Nothing known**: **L-curve** - plot `log ‖Rx‖` vs `log ‖y − Op x‖` and pick the\n   point of maximum curvature. Needs a wide enough range to show both branches.\n4. **Small problem** (`Op.todense()` affordable, N, M ≲ 3000) and quadratic\n   regularisation: **GCV** computed exactly on the dense matrices (implemented in\n   the template).\n\nReport more than one criterion when they are cheap; disagreement between them is\ninformation for the user.\n\n## 3. Write and run the sweep\n\nAdapt `reference/lcurve_template.py`:\n\n- replace `build_problem` with the user's operator, data and regularisation;\n- replace `solve(eps, x0)` with the user's solver call **with everything else\n  fixed** (same `niter`, tolerance, `x0` policy);\n- use a **log-spaced grid** spanning at least 4 orders of magnitude around a\n  scale-based guess (e.g. `ε₀ ≈ ‖Opᴴ y‖ / ‖Rᴴ R Opᴴ y‖` for quadratic terms, or\n  `eps₀ ≈ max|Opᴴ y|` scale for L1 thresholds, where larger values give all-zero\n  models);\n- solve from large to small ε and **warm start** from the previous solution\n  (`x0=...`) to save iterations - unless the solver ignores `x0`. **Exception:\n  `damp` of `lsqr`** (PyLops and SciPy): with `x0` it solves for the correction\n  `x - x0`, so it penalises `damp² ‖x - x0‖²`, not `damp² ‖x‖²`, and a\n  warm-started sweep is biased (residual too low, discrepancy pick too large).\n  Use `x0=None` for every `lsqr(damp=...)` solve, or put the penalty in the\n  operator (`VStack([Op, eps*Reg])`, `regularized_inversion`), where `x0` is\n  safe. `cgls(damp=...)` and `epsRs` are not affected (checked numerically);\n- keep `niter` large enough that each solve has converged, otherwise the curve\n  measures iteration count, not regularisation (check a couple of cost curves);\n- for early stopping as regulariser, run once with a callback that stores\n  `x` every k iterations and compute the same criteria on iteration number.\n\nRun it, then look at the plot before trusting the automatic pick: an L-curve with\nno clear corner (common with L1 terms or very noisy data) should be reported as\nsuch, not forced. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016LZ9Kv2Sqb43916ZHVmENW","type":"tool_result","content":"\"\"\"Template for choosing a regularisation weight in a PyLops inversion.\n\nRuns as-is on a toy 1-D deconvolution with second-derivative (smoothness)\nregularisation. To adapt it, replace ``build_problem`` and ``solve`` and keep\nthe sweep, the selection criteria and the plots.\n\nCriteria implemented:\n- L-curve corner (maximum curvature in log-log);\n- discrepancy principle (needs the noise standard deviation);\n- best SNR against a known model (synthetic tests only);\n- GCV, exact on dense matrices (small quadratic problems only).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport numpy as np\n\nimport pylops\nfrom pylops.utils.metrics import snr\n\n\ndef build_problem(rng):\n    \"\"\"ADAPT: return (Op, Reg, y, xtrue or None, noise_std or None).\"\"\"\n    nt = 201\n    t = np.arange(nt) * 0.004\n    h, _, hcenter = pylops.utils.wavelets.ricker(t[:41], f0=15)\n    Op = pylops.signalprocessing.Convolve1D(nt, h=h, offset=hcenter)\n    Reg = pylops.SecondDerivative(nt)\n\n    xtrue = np.sin(2 * np.pi * 3 * t) * np.exp(-t)  # smooth model\n    noise_std = 0.05\n    y = Op @ xtrue + noise_std * rng.standard_normal(nt)\n    return Op, Reg, y, xtrue, noise_std\n\n\ndef solve(Op, Reg, y, eps, x0):\n    \"\"\"ADAPT: the user's solver call with everything but ``eps`` fixed.\"\"\"\n    Opaug = pylops.VStack([Op, eps * Reg])\n    yaug = np.concatenate([y, np.zeros(Reg.shape[0])])\n    return pylops.cgls(Opaug, yaug, x0=x0, niter=200, tol=1e-10)[0]\n\n\ndef lcurve_corner(epsilons, rnorm, xnorm):\n    \"\"\"Index of the L-curve corner (maximum curvature in log-log).\n\n    Points are ordered by increasing eps so that the corner between the\n    \"under-regularised\" (vertical) and \"over-regularised\" (horizontal) branches\n    has positive curvature. A second knee with opposite curvature can appear\n    at very large eps (model pushed into the null space of the regulariser):\n    it is not the L-curve corner.\n    \"\"\"\n    order = np.argsort(epsilons)\n    lr, lx = np.log(rnorm[order]), np.log(xnorm[order])\n    # parametrise by arc length to make finite differences uniform\n    s = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(np.hypot(np.diff(lr), np.diff(lx)))])\n    dr, dx = np.gradient(lr, s), np.gradient(lx, s)\n    ddr, ddx = np.gradient(dr, s), np.gradient(dx, s)\n    kappa = (dr * ddx - ddr * dx) / (dr**2 + dx**2) ** 1.5\n    kappa[[0, -1]] = -np.inf  # ends are not valid corners\n    kappa_orig = np.empty_like(kappa)\n    kappa_orig[order] = kappa\n    return int(np.argmax(kappa_orig)), kappa_orig\n\n\ndef discrepancy(epsilons, rnorm, noise_std, n, tau=1.02):\n    \"\"\"Largest eps whose residual is below tau * noise level (None if none).\"\"

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I recommend **`eps ≈ 0.63`**. It does not come from the L-curve corner, because this L-curve has no corner. I picked it with the discrepancy principle, using your noise level σ = 0.15.

### What I did
`sweep.py` runs `fista` on your exact problem at 41 values of `eps`, log-spaced from 4.18 down to 4×10⁻⁴. The top value, 2·max|Aᵀy| ≈ 4.18, is the smallest `eps` where the solution is all zeros. The sweep goes from large to small `eps`, with each solve starting from the previous solution and running up to 3000 iterations. Re-solving three of the points from scratch with three times as many iterations gave the same answer to within 10⁻⁴ relative difference, so the solves had converged. The script saves the plot to `fista_sweep.png` and ends with `EPS_CHOSEN=0.6313`.

### Results
| Criterion | eps | ‖y − Ax‖ | non-zeros | SNR vs xtrue |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L-curve corner | **none** | – | – | – |
| Discrepancy, ‖r‖ = σ√M = 1.342 (refined by bisection) | **0.631** | 1.342 | 10 | 10.7 dB |
| Best SNR (only possible because xtrue is known) | 0.255 | 0.865 | 41 | 12.3 dB |
| Your current eps | 2.0 | 2.56 | 5 | 3.4 dB |

### Why not the L-curve corner
Plotted as log ‖x‖₁ against log ‖r‖₂, the curve bends the wrong way all along (concave). The computed curvature is ≤ 0 at every point, so no point is a corner. A "maximum curvature" rule would just return the end of the grid (eps ≈ 5×10⁻⁴, 78 non-zeros, fitting the noise). This is common with an L1 penalty and a moderate amount of noise. A forced corner would not be a justified number, so the script reports that there is none.

### Why the discrepancy principle
You know the noise level, which makes this the standard choice. It takes the largest `eps` whose data residual matches the expected noise, ‖y − Ax‖ = σ√M = 0.15·√80 ≈ 1.342. At `eps=2.0` the residual is 2.56, nearly twice the noise level, so the model is over-regularised. That is why you only get 4–5 non-zeros and the amplitudes are shrunk. At 0.63 the result has about 10 non-zeros with the true spikes clearly dominant, and the SNR goes from 3.4 to 10.7 dB.

The best-SNR value (0.26) is a little smaller still. It isn't available on real data, but it confirms that the discrepancy pick is in the right range and slightly on the conservative side.

### Change to your script
```python
xinv = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(Op, y, eps=0.63, niter=500)[0]
```
With `eps=0.63`, 500 iterations may not fully converge. Use `niter` of about 1000–3000, or check the cost history.

In the right-hand panel of the plot, the "chosen" model is the nearest grid point (0.507), not the refined 0.631.

PyLops has no built-in L-curve / GCV /\ndiscrepancy helper, so the skill provides `reference/lcurve_template.py`.\n\nRequires a working inversion (operator passes the dot-test, solver runs). If there\nis none, use `solve-inverse-problem` first.\n\n## 0. Check the environment\n\nSame as the other PyLops skills: use the project's Python runner, check\n`pylops.__version__`, inspect solver signatures in the installed version.\n\n## 1. Identify the parameter(s)\n\nMap the user's solver to the knob being tuned and its meaning:\n\n| Solver | Parameter | Objective term |\n|---|---|---|\n| `cgls` / `lsqr` | `damp` | `damp² ‖x‖²` (for `lsqr` only with `x0=None`, see step 3) |\n| `regularized_inversion` | `epsRs=[ε₁, ε₂, …]` | `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` |\n| `normal_equations_inversion` | `epsI`, `epsRs` (and `epsNRs`) | `epsI² ‖x‖²`, `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` (same scaling as `regularized_inversion`) |\n| `ista` / `fista` | `eps` | `eps ‖SOpᴴ x‖₁` (effective threshold is `eps·alpha/2`) |\n| `splitbregman` | `epsRL1s`, `epsRL2s`, `mu` | L1 and L2 weights, data weight |\n| `spgl1` | `sigma` (BPDN) or `tau` (LASSO) | residual bound or L1-ball radius |\n| any Krylov solver without explicit regularisation | `niter` | early stopping (semi-convergence) |\n\nWith more than one weight, tune one at a time (fix the others at a\nscale-based guess), or fix their ratio and sweep an overall factor. Do not\nlaunch a full grid over several parameters unless the problem is small.\n\n## 2. Pick the criterion\n\nChoose based on what the user knows:\n\n1. **Known noise level** (sigma per sample, or an estimate from a noise-only\n   window): **discrepancy principle** - choose the largest regularisation whose\n   data residual `‖y − Op x‖` is ≤ `τ · σ · sqrt(N)`, `τ ≈ 1.0–1.1`. For\n   `spgl1`, this is exactly `sigma = τ σ sqrt(N)`.\n2. **Synthetic with a true model**: maximise `pylops.utils.metrics.snr(xtrue, x)`\n   (report it, but warn it does not transfer 1:1 to field data).\n3. **Nothing known**: **L-curve** - plot `log ‖Rx‖` vs `log ‖y − Op x‖` and pick the\n   point of maximum curvature. Needs a wide enough range to show both branches.\n4. **Small problem** (`Op.todense()` affordable, N, M ≲ 3000) and quadratic\n   regularisation: **GCV** computed exactly on the dense matrices (implemented in\n   the template).\n\nReport more than one criterion when they are cheap; disagreement between them is\ninformation for the user.\n\n## 3. Write and run the sweep\n\nAdapt `reference/lcurve_template.py`:\n\n- replace `build_problem` with the user's operator, data and regularisation;\n- replace `solve(eps, x0)` with the user's solver call **with everything else\n  fixed** (same `niter`, tolerance, `x0` policy);\n- use a **log-spaced grid** spanning at least 4 orders of magnitude around a\n  scale-based guess (e.g. `ε₀ ≈ ‖Opᴴ y‖ / ‖Rᴴ R Opᴴ y‖` for quadratic terms, or\n  `eps₀ ≈ max|Opᴴ y|` scale for L1 thresholds, where larger values give all-zero\n  models);\n- solve from large to small ε and **warm start** from the previous solution\n  (`x0=...`) to save iterations - unless the solver ignores `x0`. **Exception:\n  `damp` of `lsqr`** (PyLops and SciPy): with `x0` it solves for the correction\n  `x - x0`, so it penalises `damp² ‖x - x0‖²`, not `damp² ‖x‖²`, and a\n  warm-started sweep is biased (residual too low, discrepancy pick too large).\n  Use `x0=None` for every `lsqr(damp=...)` solve, or put the penalty in the\n  operator (`VStack([Op, eps*Reg])`, `regularized_inversion`), where `x0` is\n  safe. `cgls(damp=...)` and `epsRs` are not affected (checked numerically);\n- keep `niter` large enough that each solve has converged, otherwise the curve\n  measures iteration count, not regularisation (check a couple of cost curves);\n- for early stopping as regulariser, run once with a callback that stores\n  `x` every k iterations and compute the same criteria on iteration number.\n\nRun it, then look at the plot before trusting the automatic pick: an L-curve with\nno clear corner (common with L1 terms or very noisy data) should be reported as\nsuch, not forced. With derivative regularisers a second knee often appears at\nvery large ε (the model collapses into the null space of `R`, e.g. a linear\ntrend for `SecondDerivative`); it bends the other way and is not the L-curve\ncorner – the template's curvature orientation already excludes it.\n\n## 4. """Choose eps for pylops fista on the user's sparse test problem.

Sweeps eps on a log grid (large -> small, warm-started), and evaluates:
- L-curve corner: max curvature of log||x||_1 vs log||y - Op x||_2;
- discrepancy principle: largest eps with ||y - Op x|| <= tau * sigma * sqrt(M);
- best SNR against xtrue (synthetic check only).
Saves fista_sweep.png and prints EPS_CHOSEN=<value> as the last line.
"""

import matplotlib

matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

import pylops
from pylops.utils.metrics import snr

# ---------------- user's problem (unchanged) ----------------
rng = np.random.default_rng(2)
N, M = 200, 80
A = rng.standard_normal((M, N)) / np.sqrt(M)
Op = pylops.MatrixMult(A)
xtrue = np.zeros(N)
xtrue[rng.choice(N, 6, replace=False)] = rng.uniform(1, 3, 6) * rng.choice([-1, 1], 6)
sigma = 0.15
y = Op @ xtrue + sigma * rng.standard_normal(M)

NITER = 3000  # large enough for every solve to converge (checked below)


def solve(eps, x0):
    x, niter, cost = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(
        Op, y, x0=x0, eps=eps, niter=NITER, tol=1e-12
    )
    return x, niter


# ---------------- grid ----------------
# fista soft-thresholds at eps*alpha/2 after a step alpha*Op^H r, so x = 0 is
# the solution for eps >= eps_max = 2 * max|Op^H y|.
eps_max = 2 * np.max(np.abs(Op.H @ y))
epsilons = eps_max * np.logspace(-0.02, -4, 41)  # ~4 decades, large -> small

xs, rnorm, l1norm, nnz, its = [], [], [], [], []
x0 = np.zeros(N)
for eps in epsilons:
    x, it = solve(eps, x0)
    x0 = x  # warm start
    xs.append(x)
    rnorm.append(np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ x))
    l1norm.append(np.sum(np.abs(x)))
    nnz.append(int(np.sum(np.abs(x) > 1e-3 * np.abs(x).max())))
    its.append(it)
rnorm, l1norm, nnz = map(np.array, (rnorm, l1norm, nnz))
snrs = np.array([snr(xtrue, x) for x in xs])

# convergence check: re-solve a few points cold with 3x the iterations
for i in (5, 20, 35):
    xc = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(Op, y, eps=epsilons[i], niter=3 * NITER, tol=1e-12)[0]
    d = np.linalg.norm(xc - xs[i]) / max(np.linalg.norm(xc), 1e-12)
    print(f"convergence check eps={epsilons[i]:.3e}: rel. diff warm vs cold = {d:.1e}")


# ---------------- criteria ----------------
def lcurve_corner(eps, r, x):
    order = np.argsort(eps)  # increasing eps
    lr, lx = np.log(r[order]), np.log(x[order])
    s = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(np.hypot(np.diff(lr), np.diff(lx)))])
    dr, dx = np.gradient(lr, s), np.gradient(lx, s)
    ddr, ddx = np.gradient(dr, s), np.gradient(dx, s)
    kappa = (dr * ddx - ddr * dx) / (dr**2 + dx**2) ** 1.5
    kappa[[0, -1]] = -np.inf
    k = np.empty_like(kappa)
    k[order] = kappa
    return int(np.argmax(k)), k


icorner, kappa = lcurve_corner(epsilons, rnorm, l1norm)
if not np.max(kappa) > 0:
    print("\nL-curve: curvature <= 0 everywhere -> NO corner (curve is concave in "
          "log-log); max-curvature pick is only the grid end, not used.")
    icorner = None
tau = 1.0
target = tau * sigma * np.sqrt(M)
ok = np.where(rnorm <= target)[0]
idisc = int(ok[np.argmax(epsilons[ok])]) if ok.size else None
isnr = int(np.argmax(snrs))

# refine discrepancy pick: bisection in log(eps) for ||r(eps)|| = sigma*sqrt(M)
eps_disc = None
if idisc is not None and idisc > 0:
    lo, hi = epsilons[idisc], epsilons[idisc - 1]  # r(lo) <= target < r(hi)
    xw = xs[idisc]
    for _ in range(25):
        mid = np.sqrt(lo * hi)
        xm, _ = solve(mid, xw)
        if np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ xm) <= target:
            lo, xw = mid, xm
        else:
            hi = mid
    eps_disc = lo
    x_disc = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(Op, y, eps=eps_disc, niter=NITER, tol=1e-12)[0]
    print(f"\ndiscrepancy refined: eps={eps_disc:.4f}  ||r||={np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ x_disc):.3f}"
          f"  nnz={int(np.sum(np.abs(x_disc) > 1e-3 * np.abs(x_disc).max()))}"
          f"  SNR={snr(xtrue, x_disc):.2f} dB")

print(f"\nsigma*sqrt(M) = {target:.3f}; eps_max (x=0) = {eps_max:.3f}")
print(f"{'eps':>10s} {'||r||':>8s} {'||x||_1':>8s} {'nnz':>4s} {'SNR':>6s} {'kappa':>7s}")
for i, e in enumerate(epsilons):
    tag = "".join(
        [" <L-curve" if i == icorner else "", " <discrepancy" if i == idisc else "",
         " <bestSNR" if i == isnr else ""]
    )
    print(f"{e:10.4f} {rnorm[i]:8.3f} {l1norm[i]:8.3f} {nnz[i]:4d} {snrs[i]:6.2f} {kappa[i]:7.2f}{tag}")

picks = {"L-curve corner": icorner, "discrepancy(grid)": idisc, "best SNR (oracle)": isnr}
print()
for name, i in picks.items():
    if i is None:
        print(f"{name:18s}: none")
    else:
        print(f"{name:18s}: eps={epsilons[i]:.4f}  ||r||={rnorm[i]:.3f}  "
              f"nnz={nnz[i]}  SNR={snrs[i]:.2f} dB")
i2 = int(np.argmin(np.abs(epsilons - 2.0)))
print(f"{'user eps~2.0':18s}: eps={epsilons[i2]:.4f}  ||r||={rnorm[i2]:.3f}  "
      f"nnz={nnz[i2]}  SNR={snrs[i2]:.2f} dB")

# ---------------- plots ----------------
fig, axs = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(16, 4.5))
axs[0].loglog(rnorm, l1norm, ".-k")
for name, i in picks.items():
    if i is not None:
        axs[0].loglog(rnorm[i], l1norm[i], "o", ms=9, label=f"{name} ({epsilons[i]:.3f})")
axs[0].axvline(target, color="r", ls="--", lw=1, label=r"$\sigma\sqrt{M}$")
axs[0].set_xlabel(r"$\|y - Ax\|_2$")
axs[0].set_ylabel(r"$\|x\|_1$")
axs[0].set_title("L-curve (fista)")
axs[0].legend(fontsize=8)
axs[1].loglog(epsilons, rnorm, ".-k", label=r"$\|r\|$")
axs[1].axhline(target, color="r", ls="--", label=r"$\sigma\sqrt{M}$")
axs[1].set_xlabel("eps")
axs[1].legend(loc="upper left")
ax2 = axs[1].twinx()
ax2.semilogx(epsilons, snrs, ".-b")
ax2.set_ylabel("SNR [dB]", color="b")
ichosen = idisc if idisc is not None else isnr
axs[2].stem(xtrue, linefmt="k-", markerfmt="ko", basefmt=" ", label="true")
for name, i, c in [("over", max(0, ichosen - 8), "C1"), ("chosen", ichosen, "C2"),
                   ("under", min(len(epsilons) - 1, ichosen + 8), "C3")]:
    axs[2].plot(xs[i], c, lw=1, label=f"{name} eps={epsilons[i]:.3f}")
axs[2].legend(fontsize=8)
fig.tight_layout()
fig.savefig("fista_sweep.png", dpi=120)

eps_chosen = eps_disc if eps_disc is not None else epsilons[ichosen]
print(f"\nEPS_CHOSEN={eps_chosen:.4f}")

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /Users/mrava87/Desktop/OpenSource/pylops-agent-skills/plugins/pylops/skills/tune-regularization\n\nGoal: a **sweep script** in the user's project that evaluates a regularisation\nparameter over a range, applies one or more selection criteria, and reports the\nchosen value with a plot that justifies it. PyLops has no built-in L-curve / GCV /\ndiscrepancy helper, so the skill provides `reference/lcurve_template.py`.\n\nRequires a working inversion (operator passes the dot-test, solver runs). If there\nis none, use `solve-inverse-problem` first.\n\n## 0. Check the environment\n\nSame as the other PyLops skills: use the project's Python runner, check\n`pylops.__version__`, inspect solver signatures in the installed version.\n\n## 1. Identify the parameter(s)\n\nMap the user's solver to the knob being tuned and its meaning:\n\n| Solver | Parameter | Objective term |\n|---|---|---|\n| `cgls` / `lsqr` | `damp` | `damp² ‖x‖²` (for `lsqr` only with `x0=None`, see step 3) |\n| `regularized_inversion` | `epsRs=[ε₁, ε₂, …]` | `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` |\n| `normal_equations_inversion` | `epsI`, `epsRs` (and `epsNRs`) | `epsI² ‖x‖²`, `Σ εᵢ² ‖Rᵢ x‖²` (same scaling as `regularized_inversion`) |\n| `ista` / `fista` | `eps` | `eps ‖SOpᴴ x‖₁` (effective threshold is `eps·alpha/2`) |\n| `splitbregman` | `epsRL1s`, `epsRL2s`, `mu` | L1 and L2 weights, data weight |\n| `spgl1` | `sigma` (BPDN) or `tau` (LASSO) | residual bound or L1-ball radius |\n| any Krylov solver without explicit regularisation | `niter` | early stopping (semi-convergence) |\n\nWith more than one weight, tune one at a time (fix the others at a\nscale-based guess), or fix their ratio and sweep an overall factor. Do not\nlaunch a full grid over several parameters unless the problem is small.\n\n## 2. Pick the criterion\n\nChoose based on what the user knows:\n\n1. **Known noise level** (sigma per sample, or an estimate from a noise-only\n   window): **discrepancy principle** - choose the largest regularisation whose\n   data residual `‖y − Op x‖` is ≤ `τ · σ · sqrt(N)`, `τ ≈ 1.0–1.1`. For\n   `spgl1`, this is exactly `sigma = τ σ sqrt(N)`.\n2. **Synthetic with a true model**: maximise `pylops.utils.metrics.snr(xtrue, x)`\n   (report it, but warn it does not transfer 1:1 to field data).\n3. **Nothing known**: **L-curve** - plot `log ‖Rx‖` vs `log ‖y − Op x‖` and pick the\n   point of maximum curvature. Needs a wide enough range to show both branches.\n4. **Small problem** (`Op.todense()` affordable, N, M ≲ 3000) and quadratic\n   regularisation: **GCV** computed exactly on the dense matrices (implemented in\n   the template).\n\nReport more than one criterion when they are cheap; disagreement between them is\ninformation for the user.\n\n## 3. Write and run the sweep\n\nAdapt `reference/lcurve_template.py`:\n\n- replace `build_problem` with the user's operator, data and regularisation;\n- replace `solve(eps, x0)` with the user's solver call **with everything else\n  fixed** (same `niter`, tolerance, `x0` policy);\n- use a **log-spaced grid** spanning at least 4 orders of magnitude around a\n  scale-based guess (e.g. `ε₀ ≈ ‖Opᴴ y‖ / ‖Rᴴ R Opᴴ y‖` for quadratic terms, or\n  `eps₀ ≈ max|Opᴴ y|` scale for L1 thresholds, where larger values give all-zero\n  models);\n- solve from large to small ε and **warm start** from the previous solution\n  (`x0=...`) to save iterations - unless the solver ignores `x0`. **Exception:\n  `damp` of `lsqr`** (PyLops and SciPy): with `x0` it solves for the correction\n  `x - x0`, so it penalises `damp² ‖x - x0‖²`, not `damp² ‖x‖²`, and a\n  warm-started sweep is biased (residual too low, discrepancy pick too large).\n  Use `x0=None` for every `lsqr(damp=...)` solve, or put the penalty in the\n  operator (`VStack([Op, eps*Reg])`, `regularized_inversion`), where `x0` is\n  safe. `cgls(damp=...)` and `epsRs` are not affected (checked numerically);\n- keep `niter` large enough that each solve has converged, otherwise the curve\n  measures iteration count, not regularisation (check a couple of cost curves);\n- for early stopping as regulariser, run once with a callback that stores\n  `x` every k iterations and compute the same criteria on iteration number.\n\nRun it, then look at the plot before trusting the automatic pick: an L-curve with\nno clear corner (common with L1 terms or very noisy data) should be reported as\nsuch, not forced. With derivative regularisers a second knee often appears at\nvery large ε (the model collapses into the null space of `R`, e.g. a linear\ntrend for `SecondDerivative`); it bends the other way and is not the L-curve\ncorner – the template's curvature orientation already excludes it.\n\n## 4. Hand off\n\nReport:\n\n- the parameter swept, the range and the number of solves;\n- the value picked by each criterion and the one recommended, with the reason;\n- the plot file(s) (L-curve, residual vs ε, SNR vs ε if available) and the\n  model at the chosen value next to one over- and one under-regularised model;\n- the exact line to change in the user's inversion script.\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c252cbee-9cd9-4af2-a7c8-42e240927df0","uuid":"6a9e7bd4-70e9-4197-82ea-daa1454871e0","timestamp":"2026-11-01T20:21:26.631Z","isSynthetic":true}

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I'm recommending **eps ≈ 0.63**, but not from the L-curve corner, because this L-curve doesn't have one. `sweep.py` runs, writes `lcurve.png`, and its last line is `EPS_CHOSEN=0.6313`.

**What the L-curve shows.** I ran 41 values of eps, evenly spaced on a log scale. The largest is the point where FISTA returns all zeros (`eps_max = 2‖Aᵀy‖∞ = 4.18`) and the smallest is 10⁻⁴ of that. Each run used 5000 iterations, because the small eps values don't converge in 500. On log-log axes the curve of ‖y−Ax‖₂ against ‖x‖₁ bends the wrong way everywhere: it's a flat line at the top that rolls off, not an "L". The curvature never turns positive. So "maximum curvature" just picks one end of the curve (eps = 0.0004: 82 non-zeros, an overfit model). If you use the size of the bend instead, it lands near eps ≈ 2.1, which is close to your current eps = 2.0 and too strong. Neither is a real corner, so I don't think the corner method gives you a justifiable number here. This is common with L1 penalties.

**What I used instead: the discrepancy principle.** You know σ = 0.15, so the residual should be about the size of the noise: ‖y−Ax‖₂ ≈ σ√M = 0.15·√80 = 1.342. The residual increases steadily with eps, so I bisected eps until the residual matched that value:

| eps | residual | non-zeros | error vs xtrue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.09 (your current setting) | 2.63 | 5 | 0.70 |
| **0.631 (chosen)** | **1.342** | **10** | **0.29** |
| 0.26 (best possible, needs xtrue) | 0.88 | 42 | 0.24 |
| 0.0004 (max-curvature pick) | 0.002 | 82 | 0.43 |

- **eps = 2.0 is too strong.** The residual is about twice the noise level, and the L1 penalty shrinks all six spike amplitudes a lot. That's why you only get 4–5 non-zeros.
- **eps = 0.63 is the choice I can justify.** It's the one number that makes the fit match the noise you stated, and it doesn't use xtrue. It gives 10 non-zeros, so a few small extra spikes appear alongside the 6 true ones.
- **The lowest-error eps (0.26) isn't usable in practice.** You can only find it when you know xtrue. It also keeps 42 non-zeros, which is a poor answer for a 6-spike model.

**Suggested next step:** the L1 penalty shrinks the spike amplitudes. If you need accurate amplitudes, keep the support (the non-zero positions) found at eps = 0.63 and redo an ordinary least-squares fit on just those positions. You could also drop the tiny extra spikes with a threshold first.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I'm recommending **eps ≈ 0.63**, but not from the L-curve corner, because this L-curve doesn't have one. `sweep.py` runs, writes `lcurve.png`, and its last line is `EPS_CHOSEN=0.6313`.

**What the L-curve shows.** I ran 41 values of eps, evenly spaced on a log scale. The largest is the point where FISTA returns all zeros (`eps_max = 2‖Aᵀy‖∞ = 4.18`) and the smallest is 10⁻⁴ of that. Each run used 5000 iterations, because the small eps values don't converge in 500. On log-log axes the curve of ‖y−Ax‖₂ against ‖x‖₁ bends the wrong way everywhere: it's a flat line at the top that rolls off, not an "L". The curvature never turns positive. So "maximum curvature" just picks one end of the curve (eps = 0.0004: 82 non-zeros, an overfit model). If you use the size of the bend instead, it lands near eps ≈ 2.1, which is close to your current eps = 2.0 and too strong. Neither is a real corner, so I don't think the corner method gives you a justifiable number here. This is common with L1 penalties.

**What I used instead: the discrepancy principle.** You know σ = 0.15, so the residual should be about the size of the noise: ‖y−Ax‖₂ ≈ σ√M = 0.15·√80 = 1.342. The residual increases steadily with eps, so I bisected eps until the residual matched that value:

| eps | residual | non-zeros | error vs xtrue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.09 (your current setting) | 2.63 | 5 | 0.70 |
| **0.631 (chosen)** | **1.342** | **10** | **0.29** |
| 0.26 (best possible, needs xtrue) | 0.88 | 42 | 0.24 |
| 0.0004 (max-curvature pick) | 0.002 | 82 | 0.43 |

- **eps = 2.0 is too strong.** The residual is about twice the noise level, and the L1 penalty shrinks all six spike amplitudes a lot. That's why you only get 4–5 non-zeros.
- **eps = 0.63 is the choice I can justify.** It's the one number that makes the fit match the noise you stated, and it doesn't use xtrue. It gives 10 non-zeros, so a few small extra spikes appear alongside the 6 true ones.
- **The lowest-error eps (0.26) isn't usable in practice.** You can only find it when you know xtrue. It also keeps 42 non-zeros, which is a poor answer for a 6-spike model.

**Suggested next step:** the L1 penalty shrinks the spike amplitudes. If you need accurate amplitudes, keep the support (the non-zero positions) found at eps = 0.63 and redo an ordinary least-squares fit on just those positions. You could also drop the tiny extra spikes with a threshold first.
✓ pass no-fabrication

On log-log axes the curve of ‖y−Ax‖₂ against ‖x‖₁ bends the wrong way everywhere: it's a flat line at the top that rolls off, not an \"L\". The curvature never turns positive. So \"maximum curvature\" just picks one end of the curve (eps = 0.0004: 82 non-zeros, an overfit model). If you use the size of the bend instead, it lands near eps ≈ 2.1, which is close to your current eps = 2.0 and too strong. Neither is a real corner, so I don't think the corner method gives you a justifiable number here. This is common with L1 penalties.\n\n**What I used instead: the discrepancy principle.** You know σ = 0.15, so the residual should be about the size of the noise: ‖y−Ax‖₂ ≈ σ√M = 0.15·√80 = 1.342. The residual increases steadily with eps, so I bisected eps until the residual matched that value:\n\n| eps | residual | non-zeros | error vs xtrue |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 2.09 (your current setting) | 2.63 | 5 | 0.70 |\n| **0.631 (chosen)** | **1.342** | **10** | **0.29** |\n| 0.26 (best possible, needs xtrue) | 0.88 | 42 | 0.24 |\n| 0.0004 (max-curvature pick) | 0.002 | 82 | 0.43 |\n\n- **eps = 2.0 is too strong.** The residual is about twice the noise level, and the L1 penalty shrinks all six spike amplitudes a lot. That's why you only get 4–5 non-zeros.\n- **eps = 0.63 is the choice I can justify.** It's the one number that makes the fit match the noise you stated, and it doesn't use xtrue. It gives 10 non-zeros, so a few small extra spikes appear alongside the 6 true ones.\n- **The lowest-error eps (0.26) isn't usable in practice.** You can only find it when you know xtrue. It also keeps 42 non-zeros, which is a poor answer for a 6-spike model.\n\n**Suggested next step:** the L1 penalty shrinks the spike amplitudes. If you need accurate amplitudes, keep the support (the non-zero positions) found at eps = 0.63 and redo an ordinary least-squares fit on just those positions. You could also drop the tiny extra spikes with a threshold first.","ttft_ms":2838,"type":"result","duration_ms":70686,"uuid":"9c8abd30-ce7e-4fb8-92ef-3cf202c0971c","ttft_stream_ms":1231,"time_to_request_ms":42,"first_content_frame_ms":1231,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0,"model_usage_since_last_result":{"claude-opus-5-5":{"inputTokens":10,"outputTokens":5706,"cacheReadInputTokens":91157,"cacheCreationInputTokens":17605,"webSearchRequests":0,"costUSD":0.2732314,"contextWindow":1000000,"maxOutputTokens":128000,"thinkingTokens":1003,"cacheCreationEphemeral5mInputTokens":0,"cacheCreationEphemeral1hInputTokens":17605,"canonicalModel":"claude-opus-5-5","provider":"firstParty","costBasis":"list"}}}

"""L-curve sweep of eps for pylops FISTA (L1-regularised least squares).

pylops' FISTA thresholds at eps*alpha/2 with gradient step alpha*A^T r, i.e. it
minimises 0.5*||y - Ax||_2^2 + (eps/2)*||x||_1. Hence x = 0 for eps >= eps_max,
with eps_max = 2*||A^T y||_inf. The sweep spans eps_max down to eps_max*1e-4.
"""
import numpy as np
import pylops

# ---------------- test problem (as given) ----------------
rng = np.random.default_rng(2)
N, M = 200, 80
A = rng.standard_normal((M, N)) / np.sqrt(M)
Op = pylops.MatrixMult(A)
xtrue = np.zeros(N)
xtrue[rng.choice(N, 6, replace=False)] = rng.uniform(1, 3, 6) * rng.choice([-1, 1], 6)
sigma = 0.15
y = Op @ xtrue + sigma * rng.standard_normal(M)

# ---------------- sweep ----------------
eps_max = 2 * np.max(np.abs(A.T @ y))
epss = eps_max * np.logspace(0, -4, 41)
NITER = 5000  # much more than 500: small eps converges slowly

res, l1, nnz, err = [], [], [], []
for e in epss:
    x = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(Op, y, eps=e, niter=NITER, tol=1e-12)[0]
    res.append(np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ x))
    l1.append(np.sum(np.abs(x)))
    nnz.append(int(np.sum(np.abs(x) > 1e-6)))
    err.append(np.linalg.norm(x - xtrue) / np.linalg.norm(xtrue))
res, l1, nnz, err = map(np.array, (res, l1, nnz, err))

# ---------------- L-curve corner: max curvature in log-log ----------------
# drop eps_max point (x=0 -> log(0)); parametrise by t = log(eps)
k = l1 > 0
t = np.log(epss[k])
lr, lx = np.log(res[k]), np.log(l1[k])
dr, dx = np.gradient(lr, t), np.gradient(lx, t)
ddr, ddx = np.gradient(dr, t), np.gradient(dx, t)
kappa = (dr * ddx - ddr * dx) / (dr**2 + dx**2) ** 1.5
i_corner = np.argmax(kappa)
eps_corner = epss[k][i_corner]

# ---------------- discrepancy principle (uses known sigma) ----------------
target = sigma * np.sqrt(M)  # expected ||noise||_2
# residual grows monotonically with eps: largest eps with res <= target
ok = np.where(res <= target)[0]
eps_dp = epss[ok[0]] if len(ok) else np.nan

print(f"eps_max (x=0 above this) = {eps_max:.4f}")
print(f"discrepancy target sigma*sqrt(M) = {target:.4f}\n")
print(f"{'eps':>10} {'||r||_2':>9} {'||x||_1':>9} {'nnz':>4} {'relerr':>7} {'curv':>8}")
kap_full = np.full(len(epss), np.nan)
kap_full[k] = kappa
for e, r, l, n, er, c in zip(epss, res, l1, nnz, err, kap_full):
    print(f"{e:10.4f} {r:9.4f} {l:9.4f} {n:4d} {er:7.3f} {c:8.3f}")

print(f"\nL-curve max-curvature corner : eps = {eps_corner:.4f} "
      f"(res={res[k][i_corner]:.3f}, nnz={nnz[k][i_corner]}, relerr={err[k][i_corner]:.3f})")
j = np.where(epss == eps_dp)[0][0]
print(f"Discrepancy principle        : eps = {eps_dp:.4f} "
      f"(res={res[j]:.3f}, nnz={nnz[j]}, relerr={err[j]:.3f})")
i_best = np.argmin(err)
print(f"Oracle (min error vs xtrue)  : eps = {epss[i_best]:.4f} "
      f"(res={res[i_best]:.3f}, nnz={nnz[i_best]}, relerr={err[i_best]:.3f})")

try:
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use("Agg")
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(11, 4.5))
    ax[0].loglog(res[k], l1[k], "o-", ms=3, color="0.3")
    ax[0].loglog(res[k][i_corner], l1[k][i_corner], "o", ms=9, mfc="none", mec="C3",
                 label=f"max curvature, eps={eps_corner:.3g}")
    ax[0].loglog(res[j], l1[j], "s", ms=9, mfc="none", mec="C0",
                 label=f"discrepancy, eps={eps_dp:.3g}")
    ax[0].axvline(target, color="C0", ls=":", lw=1)
    ax[0].set_xlabel(r"$\|y-Ax\|_2$"); ax[0].set_ylabel(r"$\|x\|_1$")
    ax[0].set_title("L-curve"); ax[0].legend(frameon=False)
    ax[1].semilogx(epss, err, "o-", ms=3, color="0.3")
    ax[1].axvline(eps_corner, color="C3", ls="--", label="L-curve corner")
    ax[1].axvline(eps_dp, color="C0", ls=":", label="discrepancy")
    ax[1].set_xlabel("eps"); ax[1].set_ylabel("relative error vs xtrue")
    ax[1].legend(frameon=False)
    fig.tight_layout(); fig.savefig("lcurve.png", dpi=120)
    print("saved lcurve.png")
except ImportError:
    pass

# ---------------- decision ----------------
# A genuine L-corner has positive curvature (convex bend) in this convention.
# If the curvature never goes positive, the curve is concave everywhere, so
# argmax(kappa) only picks an endpoint. That pick is not a corner.
has_corner = np.any(kappa > 0.05)
print(f"\nmax curvature = {kappa.max():.3f} -> "
      f"{'corner found' if has_corner else 'NO convex corner: L-curve is concave throughout'}")

if has_corner:
    EPS_CHOSEN = eps_corner
else:
    # Fall back to the discrepancy principle: refine by bisection on log(eps)
    # so that ||y - A x_eps||_2 = sigma*sqrt(M).
    def resid(e):
        x = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(Op, y, eps=e, niter=NITER, tol=1e-12)[0]
        return np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ x), x
    lo, hi = epss[ok[0]], epss[ok[0] - 1]  # res(lo) <= target < res(hi)
    for _ in range(25):
        mid = np.sqrt(lo * hi)
        if resid(mid)[0] <= target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    EPS_CHOSEN = lo
    r, x = resid(EPS_CHOSEN)
    print(f"Discrepancy (refined)        : eps = {EPS_CHOSEN:.4f} (res={r:.3f}, "
          f"nnz={int(np.sum(np.abs(x) > 1e-6))}, "
          f"relerr={np.linalg.norm(x - xtrue) / np.linalg.norm(xtrue):.3f})")

print(f"EPS_CHOSEN={EPS_CHOSEN:.4f}")

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I recommend **`eps ≈ 0.63`**, but that number doesn't come from the L-curve: this L-curve has no corner. `sweep.py` is in the current folder, it runs, and its last line is `EPS_CHOSEN=0.63`.

**What I did.** I swept 40 values of `eps`, spaced evenly on a log scale, up to `eps_max = 2‖Aᵀy‖∞ ≈ 4.18`. Above `eps_max` the result is all zeros, because pylops' `fista` minimises `‖y−Ax‖² + eps‖x‖₁`. Each run starts from the previous solution and uses up to 3000 iterations. I recorded the residual `‖r‖₂`, `‖x‖₁` and the number of non-zeros for each, then measured the curvature of the log-log L-curve.

**Why there's no corner.** On a log-log plot the curve is concave everywhere: curvature stays between −0.59 and −0.009 and is never positive. A proper L-curve corner bends toward the origin and has positive curvature.
- At small `eps`, `‖x‖₁` barely changes (16.5 → 13.6) while the residual grows 50-fold.
- At large `eps`, `‖x‖₁` drops sharply (8 → 0.4).

So the curve bulges the wrong way. Taking the "maximum curvature" point anyway gives `eps ≈ 0.006`, which fits the noise: 78 non-zeros and a residual of 0.03. This is common with L1 penalties, so I didn't force a corner out of it.

**What I used instead.** Since you know the noise level, I used the discrepancy principle: pick the `eps` whose residual equals the expected noise size, σ√M = 0.15·√80 ≈ 1.34. That gives `eps ≈ 0.63`.

| eps | ‖r‖₂ | non-zeros | error vs xtrue* |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.0 (yours) | 2.56 | 5 | 0.68 |
| 0.006 (max-curvature point) | 0.03 | 78 | 0.41 |
| **0.63 (discrepancy)** | **1.34** | **10** | **0.29** |

\*Relative error against `xtrue`, which you'd only know in a test like this. I used it to check the result, not to choose `eps`.

**Notes**
- `eps=2.0` was too strong: its residual is almost twice the noise level, so it underfits. You saw 4 non-zeros instead of my 5 because 500 iterations hadn't fully converged.
- At `eps=0.63`, 10 non-zeros against 6 true spikes is the usual small bias of L1. If you need the exact support, refit least squares on the non-zeros, or try slightly larger values: `eps ≈ 0.9` gives 7 non-zeros.
- The error against `xtrue` is lowest near `eps ≈ 0.26`. No method that uses only the data would find that point, so I didn't tune to it.
- The script also saves `lcurve.png` (the L-curve and its curvature) and only picks an L-curve corner if one with positive curvature exists.
✓ pass final-value ×0.5

"""L-curve sweep of eps for FISTA (L1) on the user's test problem.

pylops' FISTA uses threshold = eps * alpha / 2, i.e. it minimises
    ||y - A x||_2^2 + eps * ||x||_1
so x = 0 for every eps >= eps_max = 2 * ||A^T y||_inf.

The script:
  1. sweeps eps log-uniformly below eps_max,
  2. builds the L-curve (log ||r||_2 vs log ||x||_1) and finds its point of
     maximum curvature,
  3. checks it against the discrepancy principle (||r||_2 ~ sigma*sqrt(M)),
     which uses the known noise level and is the more reliable criterion here,
  4. reports the oracle error vs xtrue for diagnosis only (not used to choose).
"""
import numpy as np
import pylops
from scipy.interpolate import UnivariateSpline

# ---------------- test problem (as given) ----------------
rng = np.random.default_rng(2)
N, M = 200, 80
A = rng.standard_normal((M, N)) / np.sqrt(M)
Op = pylops.MatrixMult(A)
xtrue = np.zeros(N)
xtrue[rng.choice(N, 6, replace=False)] = rng.uniform(1, 3, 6) * rng.choice([-1, 1], 6)
sigma = 0.15
y = Op @ xtrue + sigma * rng.standard_normal(M)

# ---------------- sweep ----------------
eps_max = 2 * np.max(np.abs(Op.H @ y))
eps_list = np.logspace(np.log10(eps_max) - 3.5, np.log10(eps_max) - 0.05, 40)
alpha = 1.0 / np.linalg.norm(A, 2) ** 2  # fixed step: same for every run

res, l1, nnz, err = [], [], [], []
x0 = None
for eps in eps_list[::-1]:  # large -> small, warm-started (continuation)
    x = pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(
        Op, y, x0=x0, eps=eps, alpha=alpha, niter=3000, tol=1e-12
    )[0]
    x0 = x
    res.append(np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ x))
    l1.append(np.sum(np.abs(x)))
    nnz.append(int(np.sum(np.abs(x) > 1e-6)))
    err.append(np.linalg.norm(x - xtrue) / np.linalg.norm(xtrue))
res, l1, nnz, err = (np.array(v)[::-1] for v in (res, l1, nnz, err))

# ---------------- L-curve corner (max curvature in log-log) ----------------
t = np.log(eps_list)
lr, lx = np.log(res), np.log(l1)
sr = UnivariateSpline(t, lr, k=4, s=1e-3)
sx = UnivariateSpline(t, lx, k=4, s=1e-3)
tf = np.linspace(t[0], t[-1], 2000)
r1, r2 = sr.derivative(1)(tf), sr.derivative(2)(tf)
x1, x2 = sx.derivative(1)(tf), sx.derivative(2)(tf)
kappa = (r1 * x2 - r2 * x1) / (r1**2 + x1**2) ** 1.5
icorner = np.argmax(kappa)
eps_corner = np.exp(tf[icorner])
kappa_sorted = np.sort(kappa)[::-1]

# ---------------- discrepancy principle ----------------
target = sigma * np.sqrt(M)
# res is monotone increasing in eps: interpolate log(eps) at res = target
eps_dp = np.exp(np.interp(target, res, t))

# ---------------- report ----------------
print(f"eps_max (x=0 above this) = {eps_max:.3f}")
print(f"noise target sigma*sqrt(M) = {target:.3f}\n")
print(f"{'eps':>9} {'||r||2':>8} {'||x||1':>8} {'nnz':>4} {'relerr*':>8}")
for e, r, l, n, er in zip(eps_list, res, l1, nnz, err):
    print(f"{e:9.4f} {r:8.3f} {l:8.3f} {n:4d} {er:8.3f}")
print("(* oracle error vs xtrue, diagnosis only)\n")

print(f"L-curve max curvature at eps = {eps_corner:.4f}, kappa = {kappa[icorner]:.3f}")
print(f"  ||r|| there = {np.exp(sr(tf[icorner])):.3f}")
print(f"  curvature range over sweep: [{kappa.min():.3f}, {kappa.max():.3f}]")
if kappa.max() <= 0:
    print("  -> curvature is never positive: the log-log L-curve is concave and has")
    print("     NO convex corner. The 'max curvature' point is just the flattest spot")
    print("     at the over-fitting end and must not be used.")
print(f"Discrepancy principle (||r|| = {target:.3f}) at eps = {eps_dp:.4f}")


def solve(eps):
    return pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(
        Op, y, eps=eps, alpha=alpha, niter=3000, tol=1e-12
    )[0]


for name, e in [("user eps=2.0", 2.0), ("L-curve corner", eps_corner), ("discrepancy", eps_dp)]:
    x = solve(e)
    print(
        f"{name:>15}: eps={e:.4f} ||r||={np.linalg.norm(y - Op @ x):.3f} "
        f"nnz={int(np.sum(np.abs(x) > 1e-6))} relerr*={np.linalg.norm(x - xtrue) / np.linalg.norm(xtrue):.3f}"
    )

try:
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use("Agg")
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(10, 4))
    ax[0].loglog(res, l1, "o-", ms=3)
    ax[0].loglog(np.exp(sr(tf[icorner])), np.exp(sx(tf[icorner])), "r*", ms=14, label="max curvature")
    ax[0].axvline(target, color="k", ls="--", label=r"$\sigma\sqrt{M}$")
    ax[0].set_xlabel(r"$\|y-Ax\|_2$"); ax[0].set_ylabel(r"$\|x\|_1$"); ax[0].legend(); ax[0].set_title("L-curve")
    ax[1].semilogx(np.exp(tf), kappa); ax[1].set_xlabel("eps"); ax[1].set_ylabel("curvature")
    fig.tight_layout(); fig.savefig("lcurve.png", dpi=120)
except Exception:
    pass

# Use the L-curve corner only if a genuine (positive-curvature) corner exists;
# otherwise fall back to the discrepancy principle, which uses the known sigma.
EPS_CHOSEN = eps_corner if kappa.max() > 0.1 else eps_dp
print(f"EPS_CHOSEN={EPS_CHOSEN:.2g}")
